## D100: CLI settings flow through composition root, not fresh from environment per command (Package P9 carryover, S35a, 2026-05-14)

**Context.** Eleven methodology/tool CLI tests fail at S35a session-open because the test fixture's `CONTROL_PLANE_HOST_OVERRIDE=127.0.0.1` does not propagate to CLI commands invoked via `typer.testing.CliRunner`. Each command currently constructs `ControlPlaneSettings()` fresh from the environment at five named construction sites (`apps/cli/_methodology.py:149`, `apps/cli/_role.py:224`, `apps/cli/_tool.py:98`, `apps/cli/_agent.py:517`, `apps/cli/_agent.py:583`); the fixture override applies only to the fixture's own cleanup repo. The pattern surfaces a deeper architectural asymmetry: `apps/api` constructs composition objects at startup via `AppCompositions` (the `_build_default_compositions()` factory at `apps/api/main.py`) and threads them through `app.state`, while `apps/cli` constructs settings per command at the consumer. The asymmetry was invisible until the test fixture surface forced it.

**Decision.** CLI commands consume settings (and any other shared cross-command state) from the composition root, not from fresh construction per command. A new `CliCompositions` dataclass at `apps/cli/_composition.py` mirrors `AppCompositions` and holds `ControlPlaneSettings` plus the security event logger. The CLI entry point at `apps/cli/main.py` constructs `CliCompositions` once at typer-app initialisation via a typer `@app.callback()` and attaches the instance to a typer Context (`ctx.obj`); commands consume from context via the `typer.Context` parameter. CliRunner-driven tests override by passing `obj=` directly to `runner.invoke(...)` with a test-shaped `CliCompositions` constructed from loopback-pointing settings.

**Reasoning.** The pattern matches the `apps/api` composition-root discipline that S29b through S34 reinforced. Architectural symmetry between the two composition roots was implicit before D100; D100 makes it explicit. The benefit is structural: any future shared state (settings, observability handles, tenant-registry clients) follows the same threading discipline; tests stop being blocked by env-var-propagation gaps; the principle that "Secrets are read through `padhanam/config/` exclusively" is honoured at the right architectural layer (composition once, consumed everywhere). The choice to mirror `AppCompositions` rather than invent a CLI-specific pattern preserves the apps/api / apps/cli symmetry that the platform expects from any future test author switching between the two composition roots.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Environment monkeypatch in the fixture; rejected per the operator's "comprehensive solution, not a patch" framing. The patch resolves the symptom but leaves the apps/api-vs-apps/cli asymmetry in place. (b) Wrap CliRunner with a settings-aware proxy; rejected as test-only abstraction that masks rather than fixes the source-level issue. (c) Move settings construction to module-level globals; rejected as worsening testability and contradicting the no-`os.getenv` principle. (d) Use environment variables exclusively (no override mechanism); rejected because integration tests legitimately need to override against the loopback Postgres rather than the docker-internal hostname. (e) Inject settings via a typer callback that runs before every command but constructs settings fresh on each invocation; rejected because the callback pattern still pays the construction cost per invocation rather than once-per-app-instance and does not address the composition-symmetry concern with apps/api.

**Kano.** Performance. The platform works without this but ongoing test friction and the architectural asymmetry compound.

## D101: Tenant registry carries actor provenance via `created_by_user_id` column for fixture-guard symmetry with methodology/role/tool tables (Package P9 carryover, S35a, 2026-05-14)

**Context.** S30b's carryover named `created_by_user_id NOT LIKE 'migration:%'` as the fixture-guard pattern that prevents test deletes from clearing migration-seeded rows; the pattern protects `methodology_templates`, `methodology_revisions`, `role_templates`, `role_revisions`, `tools`, `tool_revisions` at S26b through S30b. The `tenant_registry` table predates the pattern and carries no actor-provenance column; four wipe sites (`tests/contract/tenant_isolation/test_registry_isolation.py:140` setup, `:166` teardown; `tests/integration/contexts/tenancy/test_connection_resolution_e2e.py:177` teardown; `tests/unit/contexts/tenancy/ports/test_tenant_registry_port_contract.py:114` teardown) continued to wipe migration-seeded tenant rows because the guard pattern could not apply. S30b, S31, and S35 each surfaced this wipe class operationally, with `make seed-tenants` as the recovery path each time. S35a's pre-write reconciliation found the schema gap and the operator selected option (a) at the session-open user question: add the column rather than refactor fixtures or defer.

**Decision.** The `tenant_registry` schema gains a `created_by_user_id text NOT NULL` column via control-plane Alembic revision `0011_tenant_actor_provenance`. The migration creates the column with a server-side default of `'migration:0001'` so existing rows backfill cleanly to a value matching the canonical `migration:%` guard sentinel; the migration then drops the default so subsequent writes must supply the value explicitly. The adapter `PostgresTenantRegistry.register_tenant` derives the column value from `principal.subject` (the principal is already required by the application-layer use case for authorization gating); production callers pass real operator/system principals and the registry persists `principal.subject` verbatim. The seed script at `ops/seed_tenants.py` changes its principal subject from `'system:control_plane'` to `'migration:ops/seed_tenants'` so the canonical seed action persists rows matching the guard sentinel. All four wipe sites gain `.where(tenant_registry.c.created_by_user_id.notlike('migration:%'))`.

**Reasoning.** Three load-bearing arguments. First, schema-level actor provenance is the architecturally-honest equivalent of the pattern already in place across methodology/role/tool tables; the registry should not be the exception. Second, deriving `created_by_user_id` from `principal.subject` avoids API surface bloat at the adapter — the principal is already required by the use case for authorization gating, so the actor-provenance read is incidental. Third, persisting the actor at the schema layer prevents future fixtures from accidentally wiping the registry: the `WHERE` clause is the structurally robust enforcement rather than per-fixture awareness of which rows are migration-seeded.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Refactor each wipe fixture to track inserted tenant_ids and delete by id list; rejected because the fixture-level fix does not prevent future fixtures from making the same mistake. (b) Use a different discriminator on existing schema (e.g., `status` field, tenant_id allowlist); rejected because status carries operational semantics (active/suspended/deprovisioned) that conflict with provenance, and tenant_id allowlists are fragile against new test-set tenants. (c) Defer the wipe guard to its own session; rejected because the carryover spans three sessions (S30b, S31, S35) and continued deferral compounds the operational toil. (d) Make the adapter accept `created_by_user_id` as an explicit parameter distinct from `principal.subject`; rejected because the indirection invites principal-and-actor divergence (the audit log uses `principal.subject`; the registry column would too if the override is unused; the parameter exists only for cases that do not fire in practice). (e) Use a stricter sentinel value for the seed script (e.g., `'system:seed:v1'`) and adjust the guard pattern to match; rejected because the existing `migration:%` pattern is already in place across five other tables and consistency outweighs sentinel-name precision.

**Kano.** Performance. The platform works without this but operational toil compounds with each fixture wipe.

## D102: P10 audit log read substrate framing — extension of existing audit context, two-destination model, chain integrity verified on read at page granularity reusing compute_event_hash primitives, HTTP transport with separately authorized routes, ingestion management HTTP API absorbed at P10 (Package P10, P10-open strategic block, 2026-05-14)

**Choice.** P10 extends the existing `contexts/audit/` bounded context with the read side rather than spinning up a new context. The read side surfaces three reads through a single consumer-defined `AuditEventReader` port: `get_audit_event` for single-event lookup, `list_audit_events_with_filters` for paginated filtered lists under cursor pagination, and `verify_chain_segment` for chain-integrity verification on a page of returned rows. The port takes a destination parameter selecting per-tenant `tenant_audit` or control-plane `tenant_audit`; both destinations share the 13-column schema (including `id` UUID primary key) per D22, D35, and the live migration state at S36 verification; both reuse `compute_event_hash` and `GENESIS_HASH` from `contexts/audit/domain/events.py` as primitives. The existing `verify_chain(events)` walker is NOT reused because it walks from genesis and a page-granularity verifier may start mid-chain; the page-granularity verification is new logic on top of the reusable primitives. Chain integrity verifies on read at page granularity; full-chain verification is deferred to Phase 2 or operator-evidence-triggered. HTTP transport exposes both destinations through separately authorized routes: `/audit` under the existing principal-derived tenant context pattern from S29b and S34; `/platform/audit` under a new platform-operator claim extending the D23 signed-token backend. The HTTP API for ingestion management absorbs at P10 from the P6 deferred carryover.

**Reasoning.** Four reasoning lines converge on this shape.

The existing audit context is the natural home for the read side because the write side already lives there per the existing principle ("Audit is a bounded context, not cross-cutting plumbing") and because the hash primitives the read side needs are already at the domain layer. P9 created a new context for run history because no run-history context existed before; P10's situation differs structurally.

The two-destination model with destination as a port parameter keeps the port surface tight while honouring the existing empty-string-sentinel routing convention from D35. A separate port per destination would multiply wiring and contract-test surface without architectural value; a destination-parameter port reuses the helpers symmetrically.

Chain integrity verified on read at page granularity makes the bet's hash-chained-audit claim a working artefact rather than a column of strings. The cheap shape (return rows, consumer verifies if they want) treats verification as someone else's job and hollows out the claim. The Kano category is must-have. Full-chain verification across the entire chain is delighter; the page-level shape is the floor. The page-granularity verifier reuses `compute_event_hash` (the per-row recomputation primitive) and `GENESIS_HASH` (the genesis sentinel for the first-page-of-the-chain branch) and adds new logic for: (a) each row's stored `this_event_hash` matches recomputation from payload plus stored `previous_event_hash`, and (b) consecutive rows in the page link correctly. The existing `verify_chain` walker is structurally inapplicable to mid-chain pages because it asserts the head equals `GENESIS_HASH`.

The ingestion management API absorbs at P10 because P10 is the second admin-grade Phase 2 substrate package after P9; absorbing it here keeps the admin-grade backend surfaces clustered. The P6 carryover's activation condition (a UI consumer arriving at P9 or P10) resolves cleanly to P10 under D93's UX-purity commitment.

**Alternatives considered.**

(a) New bounded context for the read side (`contexts/audit_viewer/` or similar). Rejected on existing-principle grounds: the audit context already encapsulates audit-domain logic, the hash primitives, and the destination routing. Splitting the read side away forces either duplicated hash logic or cross-context imports of domain types, both of which contradict the no-cross-context-imports principle policed by `import-linter`.

(b) Two separate read ports (per-tenant and control-plane). Rejected as ceremony without reasoning value: the schemas are identical column-for-column, the primitives are identical, and the only meaningful difference is which Postgres instance the adapter connects to. A destination parameter on the port methods compresses the two surfaces with one extra argument.

(c) Cheap chain integrity (return rows, consumer verifies). Rejected on Kano grounds: the bet's hash-chained-audit claim is must-have for the substrate-completion proof at Phase 1 close; verification as consumer's job hollows the claim. Page-level verification is the floor.

(d) Full-chain verification endpoint inside Phase 1. Rejected on RICE-versus-Kano asymmetry: full-chain is delighter on Kano, low-confidence on RICE without real-world evidence of chain-size demanding a streaming approach. Deferred to operator-evidence-trigger.

(e) Defer the control-plane route to the Phase 1 close substrate session alongside browser auth. Rejected because the audit-evidence claim about registry mutations weakens during the P10-to-Phase-1-close window if registry mutations have no read surface. Extending D23 with a platform-operator claim is cheap and the work has to happen somewhere; placing it at P10 alongside the audit-context read port is the natural seat.

(f) Defer the control-plane route to Phase 2. Rejected on the same grounds as D92 alternative (c): deferring P10's audit-evidence substrate weakens the bet's audit-evidence claim at Phase 1 close.

(g) Defer the HTTP API for ingestion management to Phase 2 substrate completion (the existing deferred-decisions entry). Rejected because the ingestion API is admin-grade substrate matching P10's audience and shape; clustering it at P10 honours the P6 carryover's activation condition and keeps admin-grade backend surfaces together. The Phase 2 substrate completion entry stays as a fallback if build evidence at S38 surfaces a structural reason to defer.

(h) Reuse the existing `verify_chain(events)` walker wholesale for the page-granularity verifier. Rejected on structural-correctness grounds: `verify_chain` expects the input list to begin at `GENESIS_HASH` and reports `break_index=0` for any input that does not. A page-granularity verifier must handle three cases — page is the chain head (`previous_event_hash` of first row equals `GENESIS_HASH`), page is mid-chain (verification within the page only), and page is non-contiguous (filters removed intermediate rows). The reusable primitives are `compute_event_hash` (the per-row recomputation) and `GENESIS_HASH` (the sentinel for the head-of-chain detection); the walker itself is not.

**Kano.** Must-have on the audit read substrate and the two-destination model; the bet's hash-chained-audit substrate-completion claim depends on both. Must-have on the page-level chain integrity verification per the same reasoning. Delighter on full-chain verification (deferred). Must-have on the ingestion management API absorption at P10 under D93's UX-purity commitment, with the Phase 2 substrate completion fallback honoured if structural drift surfaces.

## D103: Platform-operator principal type as discriminator-field claim on D23 signed-token backend, complementary to existing `Principal.roles`; HTTP transport for audit reader with two route trees and 403 path extension on error response (Package P10, S37 commit 1, 2026-05-14)

**Choice.** The D23 signed-token backend extends with a `principal_type` discriminator field on the token claim payload. Two values at Phase 1: `"tenant"` (the existing shape, with `tenant_id` claim required) and `"platform_operator"` (new, with no `tenant_id` and an explicit role marker). The dependency function that resolves tokens to query contexts branches on the discriminator at entry: tenant-typed tokens route through the existing `get_tenant_context` flow; platform-operator-typed tokens route through a new `get_platform_operator_principal` dependency that returns a control-plane scope marker. Routes declare which principal type they require; mismatches return 403 Forbidden with a security event. The discriminator is structurally complementary to the existing `Principal.roles` field (a `frozenset[str]` already used at `apps/api/routers/tenant_audit.py:91` to gate operator-context endpoints via `OPERATOR_ROLE`): `principal_type` anchors token-shape gating (tenant_id required vs prohibited at claim-payload validation time); `roles` continues to carry fine-grained permission markers within a principal type. The two layers coexist; `principal_type` is not an alternative to roles.

The HTTP transport for the audit reader exposes four routes following the run-history precedent at S34 and D98: `GET /audit/events/{event_id}` and `GET /audit/events` under the tenant-typed dependency; `GET /platform/audit/events/{event_id}` and `GET /platform/audit/events` under the platform-operator-typed dependency. List routes carry the seven-filter query-string vocabulary settled at S36 (`timestamp_range`, `actor`, `action_verb`, `resource_type`, `resource_id` paired with `resource_type`, `correlation_id`, `jurisdiction`) plus `cursor` and `page_size`. Response DTOs at `apps/api/routers/_audit_dto.py` (underscore-prefixed singular per the run-history `_run_history_dto.py` precedent) mirror `AuditEventRecord` and `AuditEventListPage` 1:1; JSONB columns surface as arbitrary JSON, timestamps as ISO-8601 strings, hashes as hex strings. Query-string parsing lives at the sibling `apps/api/routers/_audit_query.py` mirroring `_run_history_query.py` structurally.

Error response shape inherits from S34's D98 with one new error-code path: 403 `principal_type_mismatch` ("authenticated principal lacks the required principal type for this route"). Existing 401 "no token", 401 "invalid credential", 404 "tenant not in registry", and 422 "validation_error" remain unchanged. Registration extends `apps/api/_errors.py` (S36 reconciliation absorbed `errors.py` → `_errors.py`) with a parallel `register_audit_error_handlers` function alongside the existing `register_run_history_error_handlers`. The `AuditQueryRoutingError` from the S36 port maps to 400 (the port docstring already commits to this translation).

**Reasoning.** Five reasoning lines converge.

The discriminator-field shape forces explicit handling at every branching point in the codebase. A boolean `is_platform_operator` claim is the cheap alternative but treats the principal type as a binary attribute rather than a categorical one. The discriminator scales when a third principal type lands (Phase 2 may bring tenant-admin, compliance-operator, or service-account principals); the boolean does not. Cost is one extra string field on the token; benefit is type-safety throughout the auth layer.

`principal_type` is complementary to the existing `Principal.roles` rather than competitive with it. The codebase already has a role-list claim (`Principal.roles: frozenset[str]`, populated from the token's `roles` claim, gated at `tenant_audit.py:91` via `OPERATOR_ROLE not in principal.roles`). The discriminator does work `roles` cannot: it anchors `tenant_id`'s conditional validity at token-decode time. Today `verify_credential` requires `tenant_id` unconditionally; making `tenant_id` conditional on `roles`-membership would force string-comparison logic at the decode chokepoint and would commingle two semantically distinct concerns (token-shape validation, permission evaluation). The discriminator field carries the categorical shape information explicitly so the decode-time validation stays mechanical. Phase 2 permission-model evolution continues to refine the roles vocabulary on top of the discriminator; the two layers do not contend.

Two route trees with separate dependencies makes the authorization decision visible at route declaration. A single route tree with conditional logic inside ("is this a platform-operator call? branch here") hides the authorization decision in route bodies and makes audit-by-inspection harder. The two-tree shape mirrors the two-destination shape on the read port settled at S36; the HTTP layer's structure echoes the port layer's structure.

The 403 path extension at the error response is needed because the existing 401 "no token" and 404 "tenant not in registry" do not cover the case where a valid tenant token hits a platform-operator route or vice versa. 403 carries the correct semantic (authenticated but not authorized) and surfaces the security event distinct from the registry-miss case which is functionally a different failure mode.

The four routes scope matches the read port's surface at S36. Two reads (single-event lookup, paginated list) per destination. The third port method (`verify_chain_segment`) is internal to the adapter; the list response already carries `chain_integrity` per S36 commit 3. No standalone verify HTTP endpoint lands at S37.

**Alternatives considered.**

(a) Boolean `is_platform_operator` claim on the token. Rejected as outlined: scales poorly, hides categorical structure as a binary, requires later migration when additional principal types land.

(b) Add `platform_operator` to the existing `Principal.roles` vocabulary instead of introducing `principal_type`. Treat `tenant_id` as optional in the token payload when `"platform_operator"` is in `roles`, or use a sentinel UUID. Rejected on layering grounds: today's `verify_credential` chokepoint reads `tenant_id` unconditionally and is one of the smallest, most-trusted pieces of code in the platform; introducing role-conditional decode logic forces the chokepoint to inspect `roles` content (string membership) before deciding whether `tenant_id` is required, commingling permission evaluation with token-shape validation. The discriminator-field shape keeps decode-time validation mechanical (one field check; no string content inspection); roles continues to carry permission markers on top. The role-list is genuinely complementary at Phase 1; this is not a Phase-2-future framing. The two layers serve different concerns and coexist.

(c) Single route tree with conditional authorization inside routes. Rejected on visibility grounds: route-level declaration of "this requires platform-operator" is preferable to route-body conditional logic for auditability.

(d) Three or more route trees (split per resource). Rejected on YAGNI: two destinations exist, two route trees exist. Future destinations get future route trees.

(e) Standalone `verify_chain` HTTP endpoint at S37. Rejected per D102's deferral of full-chain verification. List responses carry page-level integrity status; standalone endpoint waits for operator-evidence or Phase 2.

(f) Inline DTO definitions inside `audit.py` rather than a separate module. Rejected on file-size grounds: S34's run-history routes pattern separates DTOs for readability; mirror the precedent. Module naming follows the precedent exactly (`_audit_dto.py`, underscore-prefixed singular) per S37 reconciliation user-question resolution.

(g) Single combined dependency `get_principal()` returning a discriminated union of tenant or platform-operator context. Rejected on FastAPI ergonomics: separate dependencies let each route declare its principal-type expectation as part of the route signature; a combined dependency would force each route body to inspect the result and branch.

(h) Use HTTP Basic or API-key auth for platform-operator routes instead of extending the token model. Rejected on consistency grounds: a single token model across all routes is simpler to reason about, and the platform-operator routes are operator-facing where token-based access aligns with the rest of the Phase 1 auth posture. Browser-auth at Phase 1 close upgrades the issuance flow for both principal types together.

(i) Extend `register_run_history_error_handlers` in place to also register audit error handlers. Rejected on coupling grounds: the audit error handlers register a parallel `register_audit_error_handlers` function in `apps/api/_errors.py` so the composition root explicitly calls both. Mirrors the established additive pattern; preserves the option to refactor to a generic `register_error_handlers` at a future cleanup moment.

(j) Issue platform-operator dev tokens with `OPERATOR_ROLE` in `roles` by default. Rejected on conceptual-separation grounds: `OPERATOR_ROLE` gates tenant-scoped operator-context actions (audit test-event at S12, registry mutations at S10) where the operator acts on a named tenant; platform-operator-typed principals have no tenant scope and read the control-plane chain. The two are orthogonal categorical markers. The dev-mint helper for platform-operator tokens omits `OPERATOR_ROLE` by default; tests that need both semantics mint a tenant-typed token with `OPERATOR_ROLE` plus a platform-operator-typed token, exercising the two surfaces separately.

**Kano.** Must-have on the discriminator-field claim shape: the bet's audit-evidence claim depends on control-plane audit being read-queryable in Phase 1 (D92, D102), and the auth model has to land somewhere. Must-have on the two-tree route shape and the 403 path extension for the same reason. Must-have on the `register_audit_error_handlers` parallel registration shape per the additive composition discipline. Performance on the DTO/query module naming (`_audit_dto.py`/`_audit_query.py` per run-history precedent); the inverse choice would still land structurally equivalent code. Delighter deferred: the standalone full-chain verify endpoint (Phase 2 or operator-evidence per D102); refined Phase 2 permission-model evolution on top of the principal_type / roles layering.

## D104: Auth error handler cross-cutting registration via `register_auth_error_handlers` at `apps/api/_auth_errors.py`; HTTP transport for ingestion management with three read routes consuming the ingestion application layer directly via Path A (extend `SourceRepositoryPort` with `list_sources` + add `list_sources` application use case); under principal-derived tenant context (Package P10, S38 commit 1, 2026-05-14)

**Choice.** The `PrincipalTypeMismatchError` handler relocates from `register_audit_error_handlers` (where it landed at S37 per finding 4 resolution) to a new `register_auth_error_handlers` module at `apps/api/_auth_errors.py`. The new module owns registration of all authentication-cross-cutting error types raised by shared dependencies such as `get_tenant_context` and `get_platform_operator_principal`. The app composition root at `apps/api/main.py` calls `register_auth_error_handlers` alongside the existing per-router registration functions. The existing single-module shape at `apps/api/_errors.py` (which the S37 brief mistakenly referred to as `_audit_errors.py`) supplies the source class, handler, and registration code being extracted; `_errors.py` retains the audit-specific machinery only after the extraction.

The HTTP transport for ingestion management exposes three routes following the run-history and audit precedents: `GET /ingestion/sources` (paginated list), `GET /ingestion/sources/{source_id}` (single source), `GET /ingestion/sources/{source_id}/status` (current ingestion status for the source). All three routes depend on `get_tenant_context`. DTOs at `apps/api/routers/_ingestion_dto.py`, query parser at `apps/api/routers/_ingestion_query.py`, underscore-prefixed singular per the established convention.

The ingestion management API surface holds to the framing minimum: three read endpoints. Upload-over-HTTP and reindex stay CLI-only at Phase 1 close. Build evidence at S38 does not pull either in.

**The architectural choice for the missing `list_sources` capability is Path A.** Reconciliation at session open against `contexts/ingestion/application/` and `SourceRepositoryPort` surfaced a structural gap: `get_source` exists as a use case and fits `GET /ingestion/sources/{source_id}` directly; `get_source_status` derives trivially from `Source.state` + the three error-text fields on the aggregate so the route projects it as a thin DTO transformation; but `list_sources` exists neither as a use case nor as a port method. Path A extends the existing `SourceRepositoryPort` with a `list_sources` method and adds a `list_sources` application use case at `contexts/ingestion/application/list_sources.py`. The Postgres adapter implements `list_sources` with cursor pagination on `(created_at DESC, id DESC)` mirroring the audit reader pattern. HTTP routes call the new use case directly for list and the existing `get_source` use case for both the single-source endpoint and the status endpoint (with a thin DTO projection on the latter). Path A respects the ingestion context's existing mixed-port convention (the existing `SourceRepositoryPort` already co-locates read methods like `get_source` and `get_chunks_for_source` with write methods like `save_source` and `update_source_state`) rather than forcing a read/write split at S38. The audit S36 pattern of a separate driving reader port is not the only valid shape; the choice at the ingestion boundary respects what the ingestion context already commits to.

**Reasoning.** Three reasoning lines converge on this shape.

The auth error handler relocation activates at the threshold named in the S37 decision (D103 alternative (i) — "preserves the option to refactor to a generic `register_error_handlers` at a future cleanup moment"). A second router (ingestion at S38) raises `PrincipalTypeMismatchError` via the shared `get_tenant_context` chokepoint. Continuing to register the handler inside the audit module forces each new router that uses the shared dependency to either copy the registration or import from audit; both are worse than a clean cross-cutting module. The relocation cost is bounded (move one class + one handler + one registration; update two import sites at `apps/api/middleware.py` and `apps/api/routers/inference.py`; wire one new call at composition root); the deferral cost grows with each new router. The trigger fires as the architectural seed at D103 anticipated.

The ingestion management API reuses existing application use cases via Path A because Path A is the smallest reversible step that satisfies the framing's "use existing application use cases directly" commitment. Path A's surface is bounded (one new method on an existing port; one new application use case; one new adapter method implementation; HTTP routes call into the application layer). Path B (consumer-defined `IngestionReader` port mirroring `AuditEventReader`) is also valid but heavier and would force a read/write split decision at the ingestion context's port layer that the context has not signalled it needs. Path A keeps the architectural decision reversible: if a future session surfaces a real shape mismatch (a UX-shaped query that the application use cases cannot produce, or read-side concurrency semantics that diverge from write-side), a consumer-defined port lands then with full design context.

The three-route minimum holds because no UI consumer has materialised that needs upload-over-HTTP or reindex. The P6 carryover's activation condition resolved to P10 under D93, and Phase 1 close is substrate-only. Phase 2 UX extends the surface when real consumer needs arrive.

**Alternatives considered.**

(a) Keep `PrincipalTypeMismatchError` handler inside the audit module. Rejected: the threshold for relocation fired exactly as D103 alternative (i) anticipated. Deferring again means S38 copies the registration to the ingestion router or imports from audit, both inferior to a clean cross-cutting module.

(b) Add `PrincipalTypeMismatchError` handler to both audit and ingestion modules without consolidating. Rejected as duplication that grows linearly with new routers.

(c) Introduce a consumer-defined `IngestionReader` port at S38 mirroring the audit-reader pattern from S36 (Path B). Rejected on duplication-versus-refactor grounds: `IngestionReader` and the existing `SourceRepositoryPort` would both need `get_source` (the existing read use case the cross-context `SourceLookup` consumer at S25 already calls through). The duplication path means real method-shape duplication across two ports on the same context with the same `tenant_id` scoping semantics; the refactor path means moving `get_source` (and any sibling read methods like `get_chunks_for_source`, `count_embedded_chunks`) off `SourceRepositoryPort` onto the new reader, which touches existing application consumers (worker stages plus the cross-context adapter) with unbounded surface that S38 cannot fully audit at one session's time budget. Path A avoids both costs by extending the existing port with one additional read method. The audit S36 precedent stands on its own context — at audit, the read and write paths landed at the same package and the separation was a single-session decision rather than a retrofit on existing producers and consumers. The decision is reversible: if a future ingestion session surfaces a shape mismatch the existing port cannot accommodate cleanly, the reader-port refactor lands then with full design context.

(d) Ship upload-over-HTTP at S38 in anticipation of Phase 2 UX needs. Rejected on YAGNI: no consumer has materialised; CLI upload covers the operator workflow through Phase 1 close.

(e) Ship reindex-over-HTTP at S38. Rejected for the same reason as (d).

(f) Defer the ingestion management API to Phase 2 substrate completion. Rejected because P10 framing explicitly absorbed this work; D102 alternative (g) already considered and rejected this deferral.

(g) Add `list_sources` to the existing `SourceRepositoryPort` but skip a separate application use case (HTTP route calls the port directly without a use-case wrapper). Rejected on consistency grounds: the existing read access pattern at `get_source` flows through an application use case (`contexts/ingestion/application/get_source.py`) per S25's cross-context surface commitment; the list path follows the same shape so the application layer remains the single entry point into the ingestion context for read consumers.

**Kano.** Must-have on the auth error handler relocation (D103 alternative (i) trigger fired; the second router that raises through the shared chokepoint lands at S38). Must-have on the three read endpoints (P10 absorbed the P6 carryover at framing). Performance on the Path A choice over Path B (reversible to a separate reader port if reconciliation in a future session surfaces shape mismatch the existing port cannot accommodate). Delighter deferred: upload-over-HTTP and reindex; Phase 2 substrate.

## D105: P11 retrieval-evaluation framing covering new bounded context, tenant-authored gold sets, recall and precision and MRR starter metrics, and no-retrieval gap closure at P11 open (Retrieval-evaluation design session, between-packages strategic block ahead of P11, 2026-05-14)

**Choice.** Eight coupled commitments. First, architectural placement: a new bounded context at `contexts/retrieval_evaluation/` per D16's hexagonal-on-bounded-contexts shape, sibling to `contexts/evaluation/` (P5) and `contexts/ingestion/` (P6). The recommendation engine at P11 reads its outputs through a consumer-defined port plus a wiring adapter, matching the consumer-defined-ports pattern reinforced across S30b, S33, S36, and S37. Second, resolution to the no-retrieval gap: the allowlist piece (adding the retrieval tool to the role allowlists seeded at the methodology context migrations) closes at P11 open as a precondition to the recommendation engine work; the per-invocation retrieval constraint threading at the ToolInvoker stays Phase 2 (the existing carryover entry holds its scope with this narrowed framing). Third, unit of evaluation: a query paired with an ordered list of expected chunk IDs, where the order encodes ranked relevance (the first ID is the most relevant chunk for that query). Fourth, substrate relationship to P5: sibling contexts sharing patterns (tenant authorship per D53, revision immutability per D31, hash-chain audit per D26, scorers as data) but not substrate; each context has its own aggregates, schemas, ports, adapters, and CLI surfaces. Fifth, gold-set authorship: tenant-authored against the per-tenant corpus at Phase 1. A gold set is a named container; gold-set-revisions are versioned and immutable; gold-set-entries reference revisions. Hash-chain audit applies. The CLI surface walks the operator through query, retrieve from corpus, mark correct chunks in expected order, save as gold-set entry. Platform-curated cross-tenant gold sets defer to the same activation condition D53 set for platform-curated scoring sheets. Sixth, starter metric set: recall@k, precision@k, and MRR, computed at k values of 1, 3, 5, and 10, plus whatever top_k each strategy uses at runtime. Latency-per-retrieval-call recorded on the evaluation result record alongside accuracy metrics. nDCG defers (requires graded relevance which also defers). Seventh, aggregation: mean across queries, computed per strategy. Comparing vector_only, graph_only, and parallel_rrf on the same gold set per D66's strategy registry is the substrate the recommendation engine reads to recommend strategy choice. Eighth, offline-or-online posture: offline only at P11. Online signals (extracting retrieval-quality signal from production agent runs) defer until evidence demands and the labelling layer activates.

**Reasoning.** The bet's success criterion 4 (the trace capture layer surfaces optimization recommendations, not just data) is load-bearing for the case study and depends on the recommendation engine being able to distinguish retrieval failures from reasoning failures. Without that distinction the recommendation engine produces undifferentiated "answer was bad" signals, which leaves the optimization-recommendation differentiator without evidence procurement readers can verify. The architectural placement follows the audience-and-lifecycle test from D16: retrieval evaluation's audience (tenants authoring gold sets, agents being evaluated, the recommendation engine consuming results) is structurally different from P5 task evaluation's audience (tenants authoring scoring sheets, agent outputs being scored against criteria), and the data shapes do not overlap cleanly. The consumer-defined-ports pattern applies: the recommendation engine becomes a consumer of retrieval evaluation outputs through its own port, matching the same shape P5 follows for trace cost via D57's two-layer abstraction. The no-retrieval gap closure is forced by the optimization claim: a recommendation engine that can only evaluate against synthetic harness runs is paper architecture against the bet's load-bearing differentiator. The cost of closing the allowlist gap at P11 (adding the retrieval tool reference to seeded role allowlists) is small substrate work that resolves a known gap flagged at P8 close and reinforced at P9 close. The "Phase 2 is UX-only" framing per D93 commits Phase 2 direction; it does not preclude Phase 1 finishing its substrate cleanly. The honest framing is Phase 1 closing a known substrate gap, not a Phase 2 preview override. The unit-of-evaluation choice (query plus ordered chunk IDs) is the minimal shape that supports the starter metric set without over-committing to graded relevance, and the upgrade path to graded relevance is forward-compatible (a relevance_grade column defaulting to 1 lands when consumer evidence demands it). The gold-set authorship shape mirrors D53's scoring-sheet precedent because the same patterns hold (tenant authorship, revision immutability, hash-chain audit, role gating); the substrate does not share data with P5 but does share architectural patterns, which is the right shape for sibling contexts with structurally different evaluation shapes. The starter metric set covers three different questions: recall@k asks did the right chunks come back, precision@k asks how much noise was in the top results, MRR asks where in the ranking the first relevant chunk landed. Together they give the recommendation engine the dimensions it needs to point at retrieval problems specifically. nDCG with graded relevance is richer but adds authoring burden (operators grade chunks rather than mark them correct) and metric implementation complexity without consumer evidence at P11 open. The offline-only posture defers the online path because online signals require a labelling layer (human judgement on production retrieval quality), which is the same shape as D55's calibration loop; reusing the calibration pattern at the retrieval evaluation context is a Phase 2 extension. At Phase 1 the offline gold-set path is sufficient for the recommendation engine to distinguish retrieval failures from reasoning failures because the gold sets provide ground truth.

**Alternatives considered.**

(a) Extension of the existing P5 evaluation context (the scoring sheet primitive extends to include retrieval criteria; rubric_application records grow to optionally carry retrieval-quality scores). Rejected because P5's scoring sheet primitive was committed at D53 against task evaluation; the aggregate's invariants assume a model output being scored against criteria, and retrieval evaluation scores a ranked chunk list against an expected set. Forcing one shape into the other either makes retrieval second-class within P5's aggregate or requires revisiting D53. Sibling contexts sharing patterns but not substrate is the structurally honest shape.

(b) Component inside P11's recommendation engine context. Rejected because it inverts the consumer-defined-ports pattern reinforced across recent packages: the consumer (recommendation engine) would carry the producer's logic (retrieval evaluation). Retrieval evaluation also has its own audience (tenants authoring gold sets) separate from the recommendation engine's audience (procurement readers consuming optimization output); Phase 2 UX for gold-set authoring would have to reach into the recommendation engine context.

(c) Bypass the agent loop for retrieval evaluation at Phase 1 (scope retrieval evaluation to direct retrieval calls, defer agent-loop integration to Phase 2 substrate-completion). Rejected because it leaves the recommendation engine pointing at a corner of the system nobody actually uses; the retrieval-not-exercised gap stays open through Phase 1 close, requiring the audit at Phase 1 close to flag it as a substrate limitation. The cost of closing the allowlist gap at P11 is small substrate work; the cost of leaving it open is the bet's optimization claim landing without real evidence.

(d) Graded relevance (per-chunk 0/1/2 grades) as the gold-set entry shape from inception. Rejected at Phase 1 because it adds authoring burden (operators grade chunks rather than mark them correct) without consumer evidence demanding the richer signal. The flat ordered list is upgrade-compatible.

(e) nDCG as a starter metric. Rejected because nDCG is meaningful only with graded relevance, which we defer. Recall@k, precision@k, and MRR cover the starter dimensions without forcing the graded relevance commitment.

(f) Platform-curated cross-tenant gold sets at P11 open. Rejected on the D53 precedent: no Phase-1 forcing function, no real consumer beyond demoware, premature against the narrow-scope discipline. The migration path stays mechanical when activation arrives because the schema is identical.

(g) Online retrieval evaluation (extracting signal from production agent runs) at P11 open. Rejected because online signals require a labelling layer (human judgement on production retrieval quality) which is the same shape as D55's calibration loop; building the labelling substrate at P11 commits to a research-territory outcome the platform does not need at Phase 1. Offline gold-set evaluation is sufficient for the recommendation engine's retrieval-failure-versus-reasoning-failure distinction.

(h) Cost-per-retrieval-query as a load-bearing metric at P11. Rejected because Phase 1 retrieval is local (Ollama embedding, local pgvector, local Neo4j) so the cost values are essentially zero; making cost load-bearing at P11 would be paper architecture against the zero values. The result record carries a cost column as forward-affordance for Phase 2 production deployment where cost is non-zero.

(i) Combined task-plus-retrieval evaluation in a single CLI flow. Rejected because the two evaluations have different authoring surfaces (scoring sheets versus gold sets), different consumers (cost-per-successful-task versus retrieval-quality metrics), and different lifecycles. Separate CLI surfaces matching the separate bounded contexts.

(j) Resolve the no-retrieval gap at P12 alongside the active testing scheduler rather than at P11 open. Rejected because P11's recommendation engine work depends on the gap being closed; deferring to P12 means the recommendation engine ships against synthetic data only and the substrate does not actually support the bet's optimization claim until P12 close.

**Kano.** Must-have on the architectural placement, the no-retrieval gap closure at P11 open, the unit of evaluation, the gold-set authorship shape, and the starter metric set; these are the substrate the recommendation engine at P11 reads to produce optimization recommendations that distinguish retrieval failures from reasoning failures, which is what the bet's optimization claim requires. Performance on the specific metric composition (recall@k versus precision@k versus MRR), the k values (1, 3, 5, 10), and the offline-only posture; consumer evidence at P11 close or beyond may refine the choice. Indifferent on the test corpus shape because it falls out naturally from the citation surface at S32 (gold-set entries reference chunk IDs from the same per-tenant corpus the agents retrieve against).

- **D107: Session log archival cadence shifts to per-package on close** (Pre-P11 hygiene, S38b, 2026-05-15)
  - **Choice.** Session log entries at `log/sessions.md` archive to per-package files at `docs/archive/sessions/p<n>.md` on package close, forward from P11 close. One-time bootstrap at S38b backfills P0 through P10 session content from `log/sessions.md` into the per-package archive files in a single archive commit. Strategic blocks sitting between packages archive to the prior package's archive file by header-naming convention; mid-package strategic blocks archive to that package's file. The live `log/sessions.md` at package open contains the file header plus any in-flight session entries; at package close, those entries move to the new package archive in the same commit that lands the package retrospective.
  - **Reasoning.** The token-discipline principle at `charter/principles.md` commits to tight working files but ties archival cadence to phase audits. Phase 1 has twelve packages; waiting until P12 close to archive ten packages of session content lets the live file grow to a size that violates the principle's intent across every interim session. The file stood at 1879 lines at P10 close, against the principle's working-files-stay-tight commitment. Tightening cadence to per-package preserves the principle's intent across the phase and cuts the read budget at every Claude Code session-open. The bootstrap clause covers the gap between the original cadence and the new one in a single commit, with `git mv` semantics preserving content history. Strategic-block archival to the prior package by header-naming is the simplest rule that scales without fragmenting the archive surface into a third axis.
  - **Alternatives considered.** (a) Forward-only from P11 close with no backfill. Rejected because the live file's existing 1879 lines fail the principle's intent immediately, not at some future point. (b) Per-phase archival as the existing principle reads literally. Rejected because the phase-close cadence is too coarse for the principle's working-files-stay-tight intent across a twelve-package phase. (c) Strategic-block archival to a separate `docs/archive/sessions/strategic-blocks.md`. Rejected on fragmentation grounds; the prior-package rule scales without adding a third axis. (d) Backfill in twelve per-package commits rather than one bootstrap commit. Rejected because the bootstrap is a one-time cadence-shift event, not a per-package commitment surface; one commit with a clear bootstrap message scales the audit trail correctly.
  - **Kano.** Must-have on the cadence shift and the bootstrap clause; both flow from the existing token-discipline principle's intent. Performance on the strategic-block-to-prior-package rule; clean and conventional but reasonable alternatives exist.

- **D108: P11 optimization-engine framing covering new bounded context, four-session split, recommendation aggregate shape, four-category recommendation set, allowlist closure at S39, HTTP transports at S42** (P11 framing, between-packages strategic block ahead of S39, 2026-05-15)

  **Choice.** Eight coupled commitments. First, architectural placement: a new bounded context at `contexts/optimization/` per D16's hexagonal-on-bounded-contexts shape, sibling to the four producer contexts (`contexts/evaluation/`, `contexts/retrieval_evaluation/` per D105, `contexts/run_history/`, and the observability surface). The context is the consumer of producer evidence and the producer of recommendation output; naming follows the bet's criterion-4 language and D9's "optimization output is recommendation-shaped" principle, leaving room for adjacent producers (active testing scheduler at P12) to feed in without renaming. Second, session split: S39 lands the allowlist closure (role-allowlist seed migrations gain the retrieval tool reference per D105's alternative-(c) rejection) plus the `contexts/retrieval_evaluation/` domain, ports, Postgres adapter, CLI gold-set authoring, Alembic migration, and tenant-isolation contract tests; S40 lands the retrieval evaluation runner exercising the agent loop across D66's three retrieval strategies with recall@k, precision@k, MRR computation at k of 1/3/5/10 and latency recording per D105; S41 lands the `contexts/optimization/` substrate (four consumer-defined reader ports, four wiring adapters, `Recommendation` aggregate, recommendation generation paths for the committed category set, CLI surface); S42 lands HTTP transports for the optimization context, the retrieval evaluation context, and the evaluation context (absorbing the P5-deferred evaluation-HTTP carryover that `current-package.md` routed to P10 or P11), plus the P11 close end-to-end demonstration; S43 reserved as optional carryover hygiene, defaulting unused. Third, read surface across the four producer contexts: the consumer-port-plus-wiring-adapter pattern reinforced across S30b, S33, S36, S37, S38 applies four times at S41, with `RunHistoryReader` from `contexts/run_history/ports/reader.py` (S33 vintage) reused as the read surface for run history and three new reader ports defined for evaluation, retrieval evaluation, and observability; producer-side versus consumer-side placement settles per-port at S41 build time depending on each producer's existing reader surface. Fourth, recommendation aggregate shape: five fields at minimum (`category` from the recommendation taxonomy, `subject` naming what the recommendation is about by producer-context record references, `text` as natural-language prose a procurement reader can verify, `evidence_citations` as the load-bearing list of producer-context record IDs the recommendation derives from, `status` as append-only state changes per D26 covering generated/acknowledged/applied/rejected); numeric confidence scores explicitly out at P11 because they would violate D9's recommendation-shaped principle by becoming chart-shaped. Fifth, recommendation category set at P11 close: four categories committed (`retrieval_strategy` driving off retrieval evaluation results to recommend strategy choice per D66's registry, `model_choice` driving off evaluation results plus D41 cost-per-successful-task to recommend model substitution, `prompt_revision` driving off evaluation results where scoring-sheet criteria failed to recommend prompt adjustments, `cost_optimization` driving off cost-per-successful-task plus run history to recommend cost-aware adjustments per the packages.md P11 cost-aware-recommendations-as-first-class-surface line). Sixth, allowlist closure scope at S39: the role-allowlist-tightening alternative from the existing "Retrieval-aware role allowlists" carryover lands as a single Alembic migration plus a smoke verification, folded into S39's front-half effort before the retrieval evaluation context substrate work begins; the per-invocation retrieval-constraint threading alternative stays Phase 2 per the existing "Per-invocation retrieval-constraint threading at ToolInvoker" carryover. Seventh, HTTP transports at S42: three FastAPI router trees following the principal-derived tenant context pattern from S29b and S34, with the evaluation-HTTP carryover absorbed at S42 to complete Phase 2 UX consumer surface coverage across the P5-P11 substrate; query-string parsers and DTO shapes follow the S34 precedent. Eighth, active testing scheduler integration: out of P11 scope per the packages.md P12 line; the active_testing context becomes a fifth producer the optimization context consumes when P12 lands; P11 ships against four producers with the fifth-producer wiring deferred to P12 as a structurally additive extension.

  **Reasoning.** The bet's success criterion 4 (the trace capture layer surfaces optimization recommendations, not just data) is load-bearing for the case study and the proposition's commercial defensibility. The architectural placement of `contexts/optimization/` as consumer with producers as siblings follows D16's audience-and-lifecycle test: optimization's audience (procurement readers consuming recommendation output) is structurally different from each producer's audience (tenants authoring scoring sheets, tenants authoring gold sets, tenants reviewing run history, operators reading traces) and the data shapes do not overlap. The consumer-port-plus-wiring-adapter pattern's fifth-through-fourteenth instances at P11 (one reused port plus three new ports plus four wiring adapters) push the methodology candidate count's stable rate forward; the pattern's structural payoff at P11 is procurement-defensible architecture where the recommendation engine reads from cleanly-isolated producers without inheriting their internal shapes. The session split's four-session shape matches P10's three-session shape scaled for P11's larger scope (two new contexts versus P10's one context extension, four wiring adapters versus P10's two). The recommendation aggregate's five-field shape balances the must-have surface (categorisation, attribution, language, evidence, lifecycle) against the chart-shaped-output risk that single numeric confidence scores would introduce. The four-category set at P11 close covers the dimensions the bet's criterion 4 needs to demonstrate defensibly: retrieval failures distinguished from reasoning failures (categories 1 and 3 together), model substitution recommendations (category 2 reads from evaluation results plus D41 cost-per-successful-task), prompt-revision recommendations (category 3 reads from evaluation results), and cost-driven recommendations (category 4 covers the packages.md P11 first-class-surface commitment). The allowlist-closure-at-S39 commitment resolves D105's alternative-(c) rejection mechanically and unblocks S40's retrieval evaluation runner from exercising the retrieval path against real allowlists. The HTTP-transports-at-S42 commitment completes the Phase 2 UX consumer surface across the full P5-P11 substrate so D93's Phase-2-is-UX-only framing has every backend surface it needs. The active-testing-integration-at-P12 commitment preserves the package-boundary discipline: P11 ships against four producers; P12 adds the fifth without re-framing P11.

  **Alternatives considered.**

  (a) Context named `contexts/recommendation/` rather than `contexts/optimization/`. Rejected because the bet's criterion 4 and D9 both use the "optimization" framing, the packages.md P11 title uses "optimization dashboard", and the recommendation engine is the primary output of the optimization layer rather than its totality; the broader naming accommodates adjacent optimization concerns (active testing data consumption at P12, future calibration loops) without rename pressure.

  (b) Three-session shape (S39 retrieval_evaluation; S40 optimization substrate; S41 HTTP transports and close demo). Rejected because the retrieval_evaluation context covers domain plus runner plus metrics across two structurally separable surfaces (authoring path versus execution path) that fit cleanly in two sessions; combining them produces an over-loaded session that risks the runner's complexity being under-served by a session also responsible for the aggregate root definition.

  (c) Five-session shape (S39 allowlist; S40 retrieval_evaluation domain; S41 retrieval_evaluation runner; S42 optimization substrate; S43 HTTP transports). Rejected because the allowlist closure is one Alembic migration plus a smoke verification, well under half a session's effort, and folds cleanly into S39's front-half without justifying a dedicated session.

  (d) Numeric confidence scores on recommendations as a sixth aggregate field. Rejected on D9 grounds; a single floating-point "confidence" violates the recommendation-shaped principle by being chart-shaped. Qualitative confidence ("high confidence based on N matching runs") derives at read time from the citation count without requiring stored numeric fields.

  (e) Recommendation text generation via LLM at P11 close. Rejected on Phase 2 scope grounds; templated prose against the evidence is sufficient for P11's demonstration purpose. LLM-generated recommendation text is a Phase 2 refinement under D93 that adds quality, not capability.

  (f) New consumer-defined `OptimizationRunHistoryReader` port on the optimization context side rather than reusing the existing `RunHistoryReader` from S33. Rejected because S33's port shape covers what recommendation generation needs (filtered queries against historical runs by termination reason, time window, agent) and defining a parallel port duplicates without reducing coupling; reuse is correct when an existing consumer-defined port's surface covers a new consumer's read needs.

  (g) Recommendation aggregate as per-category subtypes (each category a distinct aggregate root) rather than category as a field on a single aggregate. Rejected because the aggregate's lifecycle (status changes) and citation shape are uniform across categories; per-category subtyping fragments the aggregate's audit surface and complicates the read-side query path without commensurate structural payoff at P11's category count.

  (h) Evidence citations as a join table rather than an embedded list on the aggregate. Rejected at P11 on YAGNI grounds; the citation list is read-mostly and aggregate-bound, and the embedded shape is simpler. A join-table refactor is a forward-compatible Phase 2 move if cross-recommendation citation queries surface as a real read pattern.

  (i) Active testing scheduler folded into P11 alongside the optimization context. Rejected because P12 is the active testing scheduler per packages.md; folding it into P11 conflates two structurally separate packages and inflates P11 scope beyond the four-session shape. The active_testing context becomes a fifth producer at P12 close without P11 needing to anticipate its shape.

  (j) Cost-driven recommendations deferred to Phase 2 rather than shipping at P11 close. Rejected because packages.md commits cost-aware recommendations as a first-class surface at P11 per D41; shipping P11 without category 4 would leave a packages.md commitment unmet.

  (k) HTTP transports deferred to P12 close as substrate-completion work rather than shipping at S42. Rejected because the evaluation-HTTP carryover routes to P10 or P11 per `current-package.md`; P10 did not absorb it; deferring to P12 forces a third-package-boundary on a P5-deferred carryover and leaves the Phase 2 UX consumer surface incomplete across two packages at P12 open.

  **Kano.** Must-have on the architectural placement (`contexts/optimization/` as sibling-to-producers), the session split's four-session shape, the read surface across four producer contexts, the recommendation aggregate's five-field shape, the recommendation-shaped output discipline (no numeric confidence), the four recommendation categories at P11 close, the allowlist closure at S39, and the HTTP transports at S42 including the evaluation-HTTP carryover absorption; these are the substrate the bet's criterion 4 requires to land defensibly at Phase 1 close. Performance on the specific recommendation categories beyond the committed four (additional categories ship at consumer-evidence demand), the templated-prose generation versus LLM-generated text (Phase 2 refinement under D93), the embedded-list versus join-table citation shape (forward-compatible refactor under YAGNI), and the four-session shape versus three or five (build evidence at S40 or S41 may shift session boundaries within the same four-session envelope). Delighter on recommendation confidence signals beyond citation count, recommendation aggregation across multiple insights, recommendation workflow UX (status transitions surfaced as a Phase 2 surface). Indifferent on the producer-side versus consumer-side placement for the three new reader ports at S41 (the structural commitment is consumer-defined-shape; file location is a placement question that settles per-port at build time).

- **D109: Retrieval evaluation gold-set domain shape commits to revision-with-hash-chain aggregate following the methodology-context precedent; lifecycle ships at application-layer granularity for the first time in Phase 1** (P11 S39, 2026-05-15)

  **Choice.** Six coupled commitments. First, the gold-set aggregate root carries id (UUID primary key), tenant_id (UUID, jurisdiction-bearing per D12), jurisdiction (string), name (unique per tenant), created_by_user_id, created_at, current_revision_id (FK to the most recent finalized revision; nullable while no revisions exist). Second, the gold-set-revision value object carries id (UUID), gold_set_id (FK), revision_number (monotonic per gold-set), status (draft or finalized; immutable once finalized per D31), created_by_user_id, created_at, this_event_hash (computed at finalization), previous_event_hash (genesis or the prior finalized revision's hash); revisions are append-only and finalized revisions never mutate. Third, the gold-set-entry value object carries id (UUID), gold_set_revision_id (FK), entry_index (monotonic position within revision), query (text), expected_chunk_ids (ordered array of chunk_id values referencing the per-tenant chunks table from contexts/ingestion/); entry order within a revision is preserved by entry_index, expected_chunk_ids order encodes ranked relevance per D105. Fourth, hash-chain audit reuses a thin utility primitive extracted from contexts/audit/domain/events.py at the same module: compute_chained_payload_hash(payload: dict[str, Any], previous_hash: str) -> str — the canonical-JSON-sorted-keys-plus-SHA-256-with-chain-link mechanism. compute_event_hash continues to own audit-event input-shaping and calls the new helper internally; the existing audit chain on every tenant must verify bit-identical post-refactor per D26 chain-self-containment (verified at commit 2.5 by replaying tenant_a's existing audit log through the refactored compute_event_hash). At gold-set revision finalization, contexts/retrieval_evaluation/domain/hash_chain.py exposes compute_revision_hash, which constructs the revision's canonical payload and calls compute_chained_payload_hash. Canonical serialization is JSON with sorted keys, UTF-8 encoding, no whitespace; the revision-level payload shape is {"revision_number": int, "entries": [{"entry_index": int, "query": str, "expected_chunk_ids": [UUID strings in lowercase canonical form]}, ...]} with the entries array sorted by entry_index ascending; this payload is the input to compute_chained_payload_hash, which performs the canonicalization-and-hashing internally per the utility primitive's contract. The genesis revision of each gold-set uses GENESIS_HASH as previous_event_hash; chain integrity verifies on read at revision granularity by recomputing this_event_hash from the persisted revision content and comparing against the stored value. Fifth, the structural precedent for the revision-with-hash-chain shape is contexts/methodology/ (and the role/methodology aggregates more broadly), which carry chain-self-contained revision hashes per D26 even though their lifecycle is migration-time rather than application-time (e.g. migration 0010_role_tool_allowlist_pin recomputes role-revision hashes when allowlist content shifts). The gold-set aggregate is sibling-in-placement to contexts/evaluation/ and contexts/run_history/ per D105's first commitment, but it is not sibling-in-pattern to scoring-sheet — pre-write reconciliation at S39 surfaced that the scoring-sheet aggregate at contexts/evaluation/ is read-only at S16 with no hash-chain, no status lifecycle, and no write use cases. contexts/retrieval_evaluation/ ships the first context in Phase 1 to ship the full create/append/finalize/list/get lifecycle with revision-granularity hash-chain at application-layer granularity; when contexts/optimization/ lands at S41 with its own revision-with-hash-chain aggregate, it inherits from the gold-set pattern rather than mirroring methodology's migration-time approximation. Pattern mirroring follows D105's first commitment at the shape level without code substrate sharing per the principles.md commitment ("shared_kernel/ is tiny and policed: only types that must be referentially equal across contexts, never Pydantic"). Sixth, the application layer ships five use cases at S39: create_gold_set (creates aggregate root with draft initial revision), append_entry_to_revision (appends to the current draft revision), finalize_revision (marks the current draft revision finalized, computes this_event_hash via compute_revision_hash, opens a new draft revision if subsequent edits arrive), list_gold_sets (paginated read), get_gold_set_with_current_revision (read aggregate plus current revision plus entries).

  **Reasoning.** The aggregate root shape with current_revision_id FK plus revision-with-hash-chain mirrors the contexts/methodology/ precedent for chain-self-contained revisions per D26; mirroring at the shape level (not the code substrate level) reduces cognitive load architecturally without sharing data substrate. The methodology and role aggregates already carry revision hashes at the control plane, recomputed when content changes (e.g. migration 0010's allowlist pin); the gold-set extends this to application-layer authoring with explicit finalize_revision use case granularity, making revision finalization a deliberate operator action rather than a migration-time side-effect. The hash-chain audit reuse at revision finalization extends contexts/audit/domain/events.py's primitive via a thin extracted helper (compute_chained_payload_hash) rather than wholesale promotion to shared_kernel/; the audit context owns the chained-payload-hash utility primitive (canonical JSON sorted keys + SHA-256 + chain link), compute_event_hash continues to own audit-event input-shaping, and retrieval_evaluation owns the gold-set-revision input-shaping at compute_revision_hash. Chain integrity verification on read at revision granularity mirrors the audit context's S36 page-granularity verifier pattern. The append-only-revision shape per D31 ensures gold-set history is immutable; corrections happen as new revisions, never as in-place edits, which preserves the audit surface across tenant operator authoring sessions. The expected_chunk_ids ordered-array shape commits to D105's ranked-relevance unit of evaluation without committing to graded relevance (which D105 defers); the array order encodes ranking and the flat 0/1-style "is correct" semantics matches the starter metric set (recall@k, precision@k, MRR) per D105's seventh commitment. The five application use cases cover the CLI's authoring path completely; the runner at S40 adds its own use cases for reading gold sets in evaluation context (which reuses get_gold_set_with_current_revision or extends the reader port if a different read shape surfaces). contexts/retrieval_evaluation/ is sibling-in-placement to contexts/evaluation/ and contexts/run_history/ per D105's first commitment, but the structural precedent for the revision-with-hash-chain lifecycle is contexts/methodology/; D105's "sibling-contexts-sharing-patterns" framing rested on a scoring-sheet premise that did not survive S39 pre-write reconciliation. D105 is not revised at S39; the methodology line in the S39 session log entry flags the framing-versus-as-built divergence for the P12 audit, which decides whether D105's framing needs a successor D-entry or whether the gold-set's structural-first status absorbs the correction. The gold-set as Phase 1's first full application-layer lifecycle with revision-granularity hash-chain is the structurally honest framing forward; the optimization context at S41 inherits from this shape.

  **Alternatives considered.**

  (a) Inherit from contexts/methodology/ aggregate via parent class, mixin, or shared_kernel substrate. Rejected because cross-context substrate coupling violates principles.md ("shared_kernel/ is tiny and policed: only types that must be referentially equal across contexts, never Pydantic"). The structural correspondence between methodology revisions and gold-set revisions is conceptual not substrate; the gold-set context owns its aggregate root, its revision value object, its entry value object, and its hash-chain wiring; methodology owns its own. Sibling pattern mirroring at the shape level without code substrate sharing is the structurally honest cut. (The original D109 draft named contexts/evaluation/'s scoring-sheet aggregate as the inheritance candidate; pre-write reconciliation at S39 surfaced that the scoring-sheet aggregate has no hash-chain, no status lifecycle, and no write use cases at S16, so inheritance from it was structurally moot regardless of the substrate-coupling rejection.)

  (b) Three-aggregate model (gold-set, revision, entry as separate aggregate roots with their own ports and adapters). Rejected because revisions and entries do not have lifecycle outside their containing gold-set; one aggregate root with two value objects is the right granularity per the aggregate-root pattern in the existing contexts.

  (c) Reuse contexts/audit/domain/events.py primitives via direct import everywhere they are needed, with no retrieval_evaluation-context wrapper module and no audit-context refactor. Rejected on two grounds. First, the canonical-serialization rule for revision content is domain logic specific to retrieval_evaluation, not utility surface; placing it inside a wrapper module at contexts/retrieval_evaluation/domain/hash_chain.py keeps the domain-specific input-shaping inside the consuming context. Second, the contexts/audit/domain/events.py surface that needs reuse is the canonical-JSON-plus-SHA-256-with-chain-link mechanism, not compute_event_hash itself — compute_event_hash takes eleven audit-event-shaped named fields (actor, tenant_id, jurisdiction, timestamp, action_verb, resource_type, resource_id, before_state, after_state, correlation_id, previous_event_hash); calling it from retrieval_evaluation by stuffing gold-set content into after_state would semantically conflate audit events with gold-set revisions. The structurally honest cut is the audit-context refactor at commit 2.5: extract compute_chained_payload_hash(payload: dict[str, Any], previous_hash: str) -> str as a thin utility primitive inside contexts/audit/domain/events.py; refactor compute_event_hash to construct its eleven-field audit-event payload dict and call the new helper internally; expose compute_revision_hash at contexts/retrieval_evaluation/domain/hash_chain.py that constructs the gold-set-revision canonical payload per commitment 4's spec and calls compute_chained_payload_hash. The audit context owns the utility primitive; both wrappers own their domain-specific input-shaping. The refactor preserves bit-identical compute_event_hash output on the same input (verified at commit 2.5 by replaying tenant_a's existing audit log and confirming every stored this_event_hash recomputes identically); if any divergence surfaces, the refactor is wrong and commit 2.5 does not land. If a future structural-honesty finding surfaces that compute_chained_payload_hash has callers beyond audit and retrieval_evaluation, the helper's promotion to a shared utility module is the right move at that point; wholesale promotion to shared_kernel/ at S39 is the wrong cut per principles.md.

  (d) Persist hash-chain status as separate column on gold_set_revisions (verified/broken_at_row/partial) rather than computing on read. Rejected because the storage model is computed-on-read per the S36 audit reader pattern; persisting introduces a write-time computation and a stored-versus-recomputed divergence risk. Read-time verification is the audit-surface honest shape.

  (e) Mutable revisions with explicit "frozen" flag rather than append-only-with-status-finalized. Rejected because D31 commits revision immutability for tenant-authored aggregates; the append-only-with-status pattern is the existing methodology-context shape and gold-sets follow the same pattern.

  (f) Genesis-revision hashing via a per-tenant genesis hash rather than the audit context's GENESIS_HASH constant. Rejected because the chain anchor is logically a constant within the context; per-tenant anchoring adds no audit-defensibility value because the tenant_id is already on every row, and the GENESIS_HASH reuse is the simpler shape.

  **Kano.** Must-have on the aggregate root shape, the revision and entry value object shapes, the hash-chain reuse via the extracted utility primitive, the application-layer five-use-case lifecycle, the methodology-as-structural-precedent framing; these are what D105 commits and what the substrate the runner at S40 and the optimization engine at S41 depend on. Performance on the application use case shape at S39 (covers the authoring path; S40's runner may add reader-port methods if its read shape diverges from the get_gold_set_with_current_revision use case). Indifferent on specific field naming conventions (UUID column names, timestamp column names; mirror existing context conventions).
